We want a probability distribution p_i on {0..n-1} such that p*p coefficients ≤ C/n, minimal C. This is a combinatorial problem. For r large, by CLT, distribution of sum of 2r uniforms is approximately normal with mode ~? Coefficient max ~? For support length n = r(m-1)+1. Let's compute discrete coefficient max ≈ 1/√(r) times 1/√n? Wait p_i is distribution of sum of r uniforms? If P coefficients are uniform^r normalized, P(1)=1. Then P^2 coefficients are 2r uniforms. Let's set p_i = coefficients of U_m^r. Variance of one uniform = (m^2-1)/12. Sum r variance = r(m^2-1)/12. Coefficient at k is probability mass; approximate normal density 1/(σ√(2π)) times probability scale? ```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    candidates=[]
    for n in [128,256,512,1024,2048]:
       x = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
       seq = 1 - np.cos(np.pi*x)
       # optionally normalize to sum=1 or leave; maybe scale to sum=1 to avoid any overflow? sum ~n. Values <=2.
       seq = seq / seq.sum()
       # Maybe better not normalize? Evaluator scale invariant. But normalizing makes values ~1/n, sum=1, central c~0.5/n; no issue. sum>=.01 if n big.
       score=evaluate_sequence(seq.tolist())
       track best
    return best.tolist()
```

We can implement iterative:
```
f normalized sum=1
M = max conv(f,f)
while time:
   A = convolution matrix of f (A[k,i]=f[k-i] if 0<=k-i<n else0)
   res = linprog(c=-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=(0,None))
   if success:
       g=res.x
       if sum(g)>0:
           g_norm=g/sum(g)
           # try multiple t (0.1,0.25,0.5,...) and accept if new M < current M * (1 - tol)
           for t in [1,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05...]:
               fnew=(1-t)*f + t*g_norm
               fnew=clip
               Mnew=max conv(fnew,fnew)
               if Mnew < M: accept, update
           if no accept reduce t or random restart
```

def solve_direction(f, M, n, bounds_hi=None):
    # A dense
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=float)
    # Use vectorized? loops
    for i,fi in enumerate(f):
        if fi != 0:
            # g_j? Wait A[k,i] where i variable index? If variable is g_j, coefficient f[k-j]. Loop j:
    for j in range(n):
        # rows k where 0<=k-j<n => k from j to j+n-1
        vals = f[:?]? coefficient = f[k-j], k-j from0 to n-1
        rows = np.arange(j, j+n)
        A[rows, j] = f
```

```
def run_local_pn(f, n, max_time, rng):
    f=f.copy(); normalize f to sum1
    M,_=conv
    best_f=f; best_M=M
    while time<max_time:
        A build (maybe reuse if f unchanged? f changes each loop)
        Mcur = max conv(best_f,best_f) (or current M)
        try solve LP with Mcur
        if success:
            g=res.x
            if sum_g <=0: break
            g=g/sum_g
            improved=False
            for t in [0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.75,1.0,...]:
                cand=...; Mnew=...
                if Mnew < Mcur - 1e-14:
                    best_f=cand; Mcur=Mnew; best_M=Mnew; improved=True; break
            if not improved:
                # try relaxed M: solve with Mcur*1.01, etc., accept if improves
                for relax in [1.01,1.05,1.1]:...
        # if repeated no improve, random restart/perturb: f = 0.8*f+0.2*random_dir or one-sided
    return best_f,best_M
```

Time: `time.monotonic`.

Let's outline code:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import scipy.optimize as so

def normalize(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return None; return p/s

def score(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); s=p.sum(); if s<0.01: return np.inf
   # scale to sum1 for consistency
   p=p/s
   c=np.convolve(p,p)
   return float(2*p.size*np.max(c))
```
Note if p values all zero, inf.

`make_initial_known(n, kind, rng)`:
```
if kind==0: p=np.ones(n)
elif kind==1: p=1/np.sqrt(np.arange(n)+1)
elif kind==2: p=1/np.sqrt(np.arange(n)+1) * (1+0.5*np.cos(2*np.pi*np.arange(n)/n))
else: p=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n))
return normalize(p)
```

`lp_direction(f, M, n, hi=None)`:
```
A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n),float)
for j in range(n):
    A[j:j+n,j]=f
    # Actually if j+n > 2n-1? j max n-1, j+n =2n-1, within length. good.
b=M*np.ones(2*n-1)
if hi is None:
   # estimate hi
   pos=f[f>1e-12]
   if pos.size: hi=max(10.0, 1000.0*M/pos.min())
   else: hi=1000.0
hi=min(hi,1e6)
res=so.linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,hi)] for j in range(n), method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
return res
```
But bounds list comprehension okay not lambda. `hi=1000*M/min_pos` could be huge if min_pos tiny. For f normalized, min_pos maybe10, M~0.003 ->0.0003? Actually 1000*0.003/1e-6=3e9, cap1e6. hi=1e6 may make LP unbounded-ish but finite. Highs with huge bounds okay. If hi too huge, g values huge; normalizing okay. Use hi=1e6.

LP may warn; ignore.

`improve_with_lp(n, init, time_limit, rng, verbose=False)`:
```
f=normalize(init); M=max conv; best_f=f; best_M=M
stall=0
t_list=[1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.2,0.33,0.66]
while time < time_limit:
    # compute M current from f to be safe
    c=np.convolve(f,f); Mcur=float(np.max(c)); if Mcur < best_M: best...
    A build each time? We can build in helper using current f.
    for relax in [1.0,1.02,1.05,1.1,1.2]:
       Mcon=Mcur*relax
       res=linprog...
       if res.success:
          g=res.x
          if np.max(g)<=0: continue
          gsum=g.sum()
          gnorm=g/gsum
          for t in t_list:
             cand=(1-t)*f + t*gnorm
             cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s<.01: continue; cand/=s
             Mc=np.max(np.convolve(cand,cand))
             if Mc < Mcur - 1e-15:
                 f=cand; Mcur=Mc; best_f=f if Mc<best_M; break relax/t
          if improved: continue outer while
    # if no improvement:
    # random perturbation
    if stall>10: ...
```

Local polish:
```
def polish(p, budget, allow_n_gain=False):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); best=p; best_sc=score_unnormalized(p)[0]
   rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
   n=p.size; deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
   while time<deadline:
      w=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,size=n))
      # maybe block? full noise maybe too disruptive; use block:
      idx=rng.integers(0,n); width=int(rng.integers(1,n//10+1)); cand=...; cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(...)
      sc,cand_norm=score_unnormalized(cand)
      if sc<best_sc: best=cand_norm; best_sc=sc
   return best,best_sc
```
Full random noise may quickly leave basin; small sigma. Block better.

   deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0,budget_s)-5
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   best_p=None; best_sc=np.inf
   # coarse sweep
   for n in [512,1024]:
       for s in np.linspace(0,1,41): ...
       if time>... break

       if bp<best_sc: best=pol
   # if best_sc > TARGET and time left: run lp_search on n=256 with best_p and uniform

       # If evaluate_sequence inf but internal finite, still return
   except Exception: pass
   return [float(x) for x in best_p]

LP backup details:
```
def lp_refine(p, time_budget, seed):
    import scipy.optimize as sopt
    n=p.size; f=p.astype(float).copy()/sum
    M=float(np.max(np.convolve(f,f)))
    best_f=f; best_M=M
    deadline=...
    stall=0
    while time<deadline:
        A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n)); for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=f
        try: res=sopt.linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=[(0,1000.0)]*n, method='highs')
        except: break
        if res.success:
            g=res.x; ...
            for t in [0.2,0.5,1.0,0.1,0.3]:
                cand=(1-t)*f+t*g/sum(g); normalize
                Mc=max conv
                if Mc < M: accept
        if no accept:
            # perturb
            f=0.8*f+0.2*rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)); normalize
            stall+=1
            if stall>20: break
    return best_f,best_M*2*n
```

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing the given convolution-peak objective.

The core search is a very cheap parametric family that is strong empirically:

    p_i  ~  (i+1)^(-1/2) * (a + 4(b-a) x_i(1-x_i)),  x_i = (i+0.5)/n

where a controls the endpoint mass and b controls the middle mass.  This family
can make the full self-convolution much flatter than the triangular profile of
a uniform sequence.  A coarse grid, local grid refinement, and a final short
random local polish are used.  If the environment provides evaluate_sequence,
the final answer is validated against it, but internal scoring is used for
speed.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


TARGET = 1.5027


def _score_unnormalized(w):
    """Return score and normalized vector for unnormalized nonnegative weights."""
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf, None
    conv = np.convolve(w, w)
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf, None
    score = float(2.0 * w.size * m / (s * s))
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return np.inf, None
    return score, w / s


def _score_normalized(p):
    """Score of an already sum-normalized nonnegative vector."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    p = p / s
    conv = np.convolve(p, p)
    m = float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * p.size * m)


def _precompute(n):
    """Precompute arrays used by the quadratic-endpoint parametric family."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    inv_sqrt = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 1.0)
    x = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    quad = 4.0 * x * (1.0 - x)
    return inv_sqrt, quad


def _score_shape(n, a, b, inv_sqrt, quad):
    """
    a = endpoint level, b = middle level.
    phi(x) = a + 4(b-a)x(1-x)
    weights = x^{-1/2} * phi(x)
    """
    phi = a + 4.0 * (b - a) * quad
    # a and b are supposed nonnegative, but keep the construction robust.
    if phi[0] < 0.0 or phi[-1] < 0.0:
        phi = np.clip(phi, 0.0, None)
    w = inv_sqrt * phi
    return _score_unnormalized(w)


def _local_polish(p, time_budget_s, seed):
    """
    Short deterministic-style local search around a normalized sequence.
    Multiplicative block perturbations are used, followed by normalization.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None, np.inf
    p /= s

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_normalized(p)
    n = p.size

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        candidate = best_p.copy()
        width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 64)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)

        # A gentle exponential perturbation keeps the vector nonnegative.
        candidate[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.08)))

        # Occasionally mirror to help break asymmetries.
        if rng.random() < 0.2:
            candidate = 0.5 * (candidate + candidate[::-1])

        candidate = np.clip(candidate, 0.0, None)
        scandidate = float(np.sum(candidate))
        if scandidate <= 0.0:
            continue
        candidate /= scandidate

        score = _score_normalized(candidate)
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_p = candidate

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.  Returns a Python list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    base_seed = int(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)

    best_p = None
    best_score = np.inf
    best_params = None

    # Coarse shape grid.  These brackets include the strong “U-shaped in phi”
    # sequences that reduce the convolution peak below the uniform baseline.
    coarse_a = [0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.0]
    coarse_b = [0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 1.8, 2.5, 3.5, 4.5, 6.0]

    # Stage 1: coarse sweep on relatively small lengths to identify good shapes.
    coarse_n_values = [512, 1024]
    coarse_candidates = []

    for n in coarse_n_values:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        inv_sqrt, quad = _precompute(n)
        for a in coarse_a:
            for b in coarse_b:
                if a < 0.0 or b < 0.0:
                    continue
                if a == 0.0 and b == 0.0:
                    continue
                score, seq = _score_shape(n, a, b, inv_sqrt, quad)
                if seq is None:
                    continue
                coarse_candidates.append((score, a, b, n, seq))
                if score < best_score:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = seq
                    best_params = (a, b, n)
        if coarse_n_values and n == coarse_n_values[0]:
            print(f"[coarse-n={n}] best so far: {best_score:.10f} params={best_params}")

    # Sort by score and keep a handful of promising shapes.
    coarse_candidates = [c for c in coarse_candidates if np.isfinite(c[0])]
    coarse_candidates.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
    top_shapes = []
    seen = set()
    for score, a, b, n, seq in coarse_candidates:
        key = (round(a, 3), round(b, 3))
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        top_shapes.append((a, b))
        if len(top_shapes) >= 6:
            break

    if not top_shapes:
        # Absolute fallback: one-sided square-root profile, a known valid baseline.
        n = 512
        inv_sqrt, quad = _precompute(n)
        score, seq = _score_shape(n, 1.0, 1.0, inv_sqrt, quad)
        if seq is not None:
            best_p = seq
            best_score = score

    # Stage 2: refine promising shapes on n=1024.
    refined_candidates = []
    if top_shapes:
        n_ref = 1024
        inv_sqrt, quad = _precompute(n_ref)
        for a0, b0 in top_shapes:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            a_lo = max(0.0, a0 - 0.08)
            a_hi = min(1.0, a0 + 0.08)
            b_lo = max(0.02, b0 - 1.2)
            b_hi = max(b_lo + 0.1, b0 + 1.2)

            a_grid = np.linspace(a_lo, a_hi, 13)
            b_grid = np.linspace(b_lo, b_hi, 13)

            for a in a_grid:
                a = float(a)
                for b in b_grid:
                    b = float(b)
                    score, seq = _score_shape(n_ref, a, b, inv_sqrt, quad)
                    if seq is None:
                        continue
                    refined_candidates.append((score, a, b, seq))
                    if score < best_score:
                        best_score = score
                        best_p = seq
                        best_params = (a, b, n_ref)

    refined_candidates = [c for c in refined_candidates if np.isfinite(c[0])]
    refined_candidates.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])

    # Stage 3: evaluate the best refined shapes on larger lengths.
    final_n_values = []
    remaining_est = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining_est > 10.0:
        final_n_values.append(2048)
    if remaining_est > 35.0:
        final_n_values.append(3072)
    if remaining_est > 90.0:
        final_n_values.append(4096)

    # If nothing larger was allowed, still refine the exact best shape at 1024.
    if not final_n_values and refined_candidates:
        final_n_values.append(1024)

    top_refined = []
    seen_shape = set()
    for score, a, b, seq in refined_candidates:
        key = (round(a, 4), round(b, 4))
        if key in seen_shape:
            continue
        seen_shape.add(key)
        top_refined.append((a, b))
        if len(top_refined) >= 16:
            break

    for n in final_n_values:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        inv_sqrt, quad = _precompute(n)
        for a, b in top_refined:
            score, seq = _score_shape(n, a, b, inv_sqrt, quad)
            if seq is None:
                continue
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_p = seq
                best_params = (a, b, n)
        print(f"[final-n={n}] best score={best_score:.10f} params={best_params}")

    # If the search completely failed, use a safe sequence.
    if best_p is None:
        n = 512
        inv_sqrt, quad = _precompute(n)
        best_score, best_p = _score_shape(n, 1.0, 1.0, inv_sqrt, quad)
        if best_p is None:
            best_p = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64)
            best_score = 2.0

    # Stage 4: short local polish if there is enough time and length is reasonable.
    if best_p is not None and best_p.size <= 4096 and deadline - time.monotonic() > 25.0:
        polish_budget = min(35.0, max(5.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.25))
        pol_p, pol_score = _local_polish(best_p, polish_budget, seed=base_seed + 17)
        if pol_p is not None and np.isfinite(pol_score) and pol_score < best_score:
            best_p = pol_p
            best_score = pol_score
            print(f"[polish] improved to {best_score:.10f}")

    # Final validation using the official evaluator, if available.
    # We do not import it; it is assumed to be in the global namespace.
    try:
        final_score = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_p])
        if np.isfinite(final_score):
            print(f"[official] final score={final_score:.10f}")
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[official-eval-error] {exc}")

    return [float(x) for x in best_p]
```
